ALGEBRA WORD PROBLEMS WITH SOLUTIONS
1000 problems from 216 problem types in 55 categories.

Format per entry: ID [Category] / Problem / Let / Equation / Solve / Answer, separated by a line of '-'.

#0001 [Age]
Problem: Wren is 4 times as old as Jamal. In 5 years, the sum of their ages will be 105. How old is each now?
Let: x = Jamal's age now; Wren = 4x
Equation: (x + 5) + (4x + 5) = 105
Solve: 5x + 10 = 105 → x = 19
Answer: Jamal is 19, Wren is 76
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0002 [Age]
Problem: Wes is 13 years older than Theo. The sum of their ages is 85. How old is each?
Let: x = Theo's age
Equation: x + (x + 13) = 85
Solve: 2x = 72 → x = 36
Answer: Theo is 36, Wes is 49
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0003 [Age]
Problem: Ben is 8 years older than Sam. 32 years ago, Ben was twice as old as Sam. How old is each now?
Let: x = Sam's age now
Equation: (x + 8) − 32 = 2(x − 32)
Solve: x + -24 = 2x − 64 → x = 40
Answer: Sam is 40, Ben is 48
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0004 [Age]
Problem: Dana is 2 times as old as Aiden. In 6 years, the sum of their ages will be 33. How old is each now?
Let: x = Aiden's age now; Dana = 2x
Equation: (x + 6) + (2x + 6) = 33
Solve: 3x + 12 = 33 → x = 7
Answer: Aiden is 7, Dana is 14
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0010 [Average]
Problem: The mean of 4 numbers is 41. 3 of them are 56, 32, 17. What is the missing number?
Let: x = missing number
Equation: (56 + 32 + 17 + x)/4 = 41
Solve: x/4 = 14.75 → x = 14.75 × 4 = 59
Answer: 59
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0011 [Average]
Problem: Ava scored 87, 71, 73, 89, 97 on 5 tests. What score is needed on the next test to average 86?
Let: x = next score
Equation: (87 + 71 + 73 + 89 + 97 + x) / 6 = 86
Solve: 417 + x = 516 → x = 99
Answer: 99
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0019 [Average (weighted)]
Problem: Class A has 13 students averaging 75. Class B averages 93. Together the classes average 84. How many students are in class B?
Let: n = students in class B
Equation: (13×75 + 93n)/(13 + n) = 84
Solve: Multiply both sides by the denominator and collect terms → n = 13
Answer: 13 students
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0020 [Average (weighted)]
Problem: A course grade is 75% coursework and 25% final exam. Elena has a coursework average of 96. What final exam score gives an overall grade of 86?
Let: f = final exam score
Equation: 0.75(96) + 0.25f = 86
Solve: f/4 = 14 → f = 14 × 4 = 56
Answer: 56
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Equation: 0.6(79) + 0.4f = 85
Solve: 0.4f = 37.6 → f = 94
Answer: 94

#0029 [Break-even]
Problem: Electric utility plan A costs $38.00 plus $0.15 per kWh. Plan B costs $26.50 plus $0.20 per kWh. For how many kWhs do they cost the same?
Let: x = number of kWhs
Equation: 38 + 0.15x = 26.5 + 0.2x
Solve: 0.05x = 11.5 → x = 230
Answer: 230 kWhs
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0030 [Break-even]
Problem: Service A has a $160 sign-up fee and costs $5/month. Service B has no fee and costs $15/month. After how many months is the total cost the same?
Let: n = months
Equation: 160 + 5n = 15n
Solve: 10n = 160 → n = 16
Answer: 16 months
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0031 [Break-even]
Problem: Job A pays $1000 a month plus 2% commission. Job B pays $490 a month plus 5% commission. At what monthly sales total do both pay the same?
Let: s = monthly sales
Equation: 1000 + 0.02s = 490 + 0.05s
Solve: 0.03s = 510 → s = 17000
Answer: $17,000.00
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0032 [Break-even]
Problem: A food truck pays $350 in daily costs, and each meal costs $1 in ingredients. Meals sell for $8. How many meals a day cover the costs?
Let: n = number of meals
Equation: 8n = 350 + n
Solve: 7n = 350 → n = 50
Answer: 50 meals
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0033 [Break-even]
Problem: Buying a pressure washer costs $500. Renting one costs $50 once plus $50 per use. After how many uses does renting cost the same as buying?
Let: n = uses
Equation: 50 + 50n = 500
Solve: 50n = 450 → n = 9
Answer: 9 uses
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0034 [Break-even]
Problem: Job A pays $2200 a month plus 4% commission. Job B pays $160 a month plus 8% commission. At what monthly sales total do both pay the same?
Let: s = monthly sales
Equation: 2200 + 0.04s = 160 + 0.08s
Solve: s/25 = 2040 → s = 2040 × 25 = 51000
Answer: $51,000.00
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0053 [Clock]
Problem: At what time between 1:00 and 2:00 are the hour and minute hands of a clock exactly together?
Let: m = minutes after 1:00
Equation: 6m = 30 + 0.5m
Solve: 5.5m = 30 → m = 60/11
Answer: 60/11 ≈ 5.45 minutes after 1:00
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0057 [Coins]
Problem: Pablo has 45 coins, all nickels and quarters, worth $7.45 in total. How many of each coin are there?
Let: x = number of nickels; 45 − x = number of quarters
Equation: 5x + 25(45 − x) = 745
Solve: 20x = 380 → x = 19
Answer: 19 nickels and 26 quarters
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0058 [Coins]
Problem: A jar has quarters, dimes, and nickels worth $2.65. There are 4 more dimes than quarters and twice as many nickels as quarters. How many of each coin?
Let: q = quarters
Equation: 25q + 10(q + 4) + 5(2q) = 265
Solve: 45q = 225 → q = 5
Answer: 5 quarters, 9 dimes, 10 nickels
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0067 [Consecutive integers]
Problem: Of three consecutive even integers, the sum of the smallest and largest is 8. Find all three.
Let: x = smallest
Equation: x + (x + 4) = 8
Solve: 2x = 4 → x = 2
Answer: 2, 4, 6
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0068 [Consecutive integers]
Problem: The average of 5 consecutive integers is 50. What is the smallest?
Let: x = smallest
Equation: (x + (x + 4))/2 = 50
Solve: Collect like terms → x = 48
Answer: 48 (the integers are 48 to 52)
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0069 [Consecutive integers]
Problem: Three consecutive integers are such that 3 times the smallest is 4 more than the largest. Find the integers.
Let: x = smallest
Equation: 3x = (x + 2) + 4
Solve: 2x = 6 → x = 3
Answer: 3, 4, 5
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Solve: 3x + 3 = 120 → 3x = 117 → x = 39
Answer: 39, 40, 41

#0083 [Consecutive integers]
Problem: Of three consecutive even integers, the sum of the smallest and largest is 104. Find all three.
Let: x = smallest
Equation: x + (x + 4) = 104
Solve: 2x = 100 → x = 50
Answer: 50, 52, 54
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0084 [Consecutive integers]
Problem: The sum of 4 consecutive even integers is 244. What are the integers?
Let: x = the smallest integer
Equation: x + (x + 2) + (x + 4) + (x + 6) = 244
Solve: 4x + 12 = 244 → 4x = 232 → x = 58
Answer: 58, 60, 62, 64
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0085 [Consecutive integers]
Problem: The average of 7 consecutive integers is 50. What is the smallest?
Let: x = smallest
Equation: (x + (x + 6))/2 = 50
Solve: Collect like terms → x = 47
Answer: 47 (the integers are 47 to 53)
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0086 [Current / wind]
Problem: A boat travels 34 km downstream in 2 hours and 35 km upstream in 5 hours. Find the speed of the boat in still water and the speed of the current.
Let: b = boat speed, c = current speed
Equation: b + c = 17;  b − c = 7
Solve: 2b = 24 → b = 12, c = 5
Answer: Boat 12 km/h, current 5 km/h
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0087 [Current / wind]
Problem: A plane flies 540 miles with the wind in 3 hours. Flying back against the same wind takes 4.5 hours. Find the plane's speed in still air and the wind speed.
Let: p = plane speed, w = wind speed
Equation: p + w = 180;  p − w = 120
Solve: 2p = 300 → p = 150, w = 30
Answer: Plane 150 mph, wind 30 mph
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0095 [Digits]
Problem: In a two-digit number, the units digit is 3 times the tens digit. The number is 27 more than the sum of its digits. Find the number.
Let: t = tens digit; units digit = 3t
Equation: (10t + 3t) − (t + 3t) = 27
Solve: 9t = 27 → t = 3
Answer: 39
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0096 [Digits]
Problem: The digits of a two-digit number add up to 11. When the digits are reversed, the new number is 63 less than the original. Find the original number.
Let: t = tens digit, u = units digit
Equation: t + u = 11;  (10u + t) − (10t + u) = -63
Solve: 9(u − t) = -63 → u − t = -7; with t + u = 11: t = 9, u = 2
Answer: 92
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0104 [Digits]

#0105 [Direct variation]
Problem: A spring stretches 2.5 cm under a 20 N force. Stretch varies directly with force. How far will it stretch under 40 N?
Let: x = stretch
Equation: 2.5/20 = x/40
Solve: x/40 = 0.125 → x = 0.125 × 40 = 5
Answer: 5 cm
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0106 [Direct variation]
Problem: Pay varies directly with hours worked. Kira earns $900.00 for 40 hours. How much for 42 hours?
Let: k = hourly rate
Equation: 900 = k·40; pay = k·42
Solve: k = 22.5 → pay = 945
Answer: $945.00
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0115 [Distance (average speed)]
Problem: Kofi drove 1 hour at 30 mph and then some hours at 55 mph. The average speed for the whole trip was 50 mph. How long was the second part?
Let: t = hours at 55 mph
Equation: (30×1 + 55t)/(1 + t) = 50
Solve: Multiply both sides by the denominator and collect terms → t = 4
Answer: 4 hours
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0116 [Distance (average speed)]
Problem: A car goes from A to B at 60 km/h. How fast must it return so that the average speed for the round trip is 30 km/h?
Let: v = return speed
Equation: 2/(1/60 + 1/v) = 30
Solve: Rearrange: 1/v = 2/30 − 1/60 → v = 20
Answer: 20 km/h
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0117 [Distance (average speed)]
Problem: Aiden drove 2 hours at 60 mph and then some hours at 45 mph. The average speed for the whole trip was 55 mph. How long was the second part?
Let: t = hours at 45 mph
Equation: (60×2 + 45t)/(2 + t) = 55
Solve: Multiply both sides by the denominator and collect terms → t = 1
Answer: 1 hour
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0125 [Distance (catch-up)]
Problem: Two runners start together on a 400 m track, one at 5 m/s and the other at 7 m/s. After how many seconds does the faster one lap the slower one?
Let: t = seconds
Equation: 7t − 5t = 400
Solve: 2t = 400 → t = 200
Answer: 200 seconds
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0126 [Distance (catch-up)]
Problem: Caleb leaves at 39 mph. 2 hours later, Aiden follows the same route at 63 mph. How long after Aiden leaves will Aiden catch up?
Let: t = hours Aiden drives
Equation: 63t = 39(t + 2)
Solve: 24t = 78 → t = 3.25
Answer: 3.25 hours
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0127 [Distance (catch-up)]
Problem: Two runners start together on a 250 m track, one at 6 m/s and the other at 7 m/s. After how many seconds does the faster one lap the slower one?
Let: t = seconds
Equation: 7t − 6t = 250
Solve: Collect like terms → t = 250
Answer: 250 seconds
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0140 [Distance (round trip)]
Problem: Tara drove to a lake at 45 mph and returned on the same road at 36 mph. The round trip took 8 hours. How far is the lake?
Let: d = one-way distance
Equation: d/45 + d/36 = 8
Solve: Multiply by 180 → d = 160
Answer: 160 miles
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0141 [Distance (round trip)]
Problem: A cyclist rides 225 km to a town and returns 5 km/h faster. The whole trip takes 9.5 hours. How fast was the outbound ride?
Let: s = outbound speed
Equation: 225/s + 225/(s + 5) = 9.5
Solve: Clear denominators → quadratic → s = 45 (reject negative)
Answer: 45 km/h
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0149 [Distance (toward each other)]
Problem: Carla and Jamal live 391 miles apart. Carla drives toward Jamal at 67 mph. 1 hour later, Jamal starts driving toward Carla at 41 mph. How long after Jamal leaves do they meet?
Let: t = hours Jamal drives
Equation: 67(t + 1) + 41t = 391
Solve: 108t = 324 → t = 3
Answer: 3 hours
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0159 [Distance (two legs)]
Problem: Pablo walked at 4 km/h and then rode a bus at 40 km/h. The whole trip was 13 km and took 1 hours. How long was the walk?
Let: t = walking time (h)
Equation: 4t + 40(1 − t) = 13
Solve: 36t = 27 → t = 0.75
Answer: 0.75 h walking, 0.25 h on the bus
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0160 [Distance (two legs)]
Problem: Lena walked at 5 km/h and then rode a bus at 36 km/h. The whole trip was 38.5 km and took 1.5 hours. How long was the walk?
Let: t = walking time (h)
Equation: 5t + 36(1.5 − t) = 38.5
Solve: 31t = 15.5 → t = 0.5
Answer: 0.5 h walking, 1 h on the bus
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Answer: 0.5 h walking, 1.5 h on the bus

#0164 [Exponential]
Problem: A substance has a half-life of 2 years. How long until 200 g decays to 25 g?
Let: t = years
Equation: 200 × (1/2)^(t/2) = 25
Solve: (1/2)^(t/2) = 1/8 → t/2 = 3 → t = 6
Answer: 6 years
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0165 [Exponential]
Problem: A bacteria culture starts with 1000 cells and doubles every 20 minutes. When will it reach 4,000 cells?
Let: t = minutes
Equation: 1000 × 2^(t/20) = 4000
Solve: 2^(t/20) = 4 = 2^2 → t/20 = 2 → t = 40
Answer: 40 minutes
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0166 [Exponential]

Let: t = days
Equation: 50 × 3^(t/5) = 450
Solve: 3^(t/5) = 9 → t/5 = 2 → t = 10
Answer: 10 days

#0211 [Fractions]
Problem: A puzzle: a man spent 1/6 of his life as a boy, 1/12 more as a youth, and 1/7 more before marrying. 5 years later a son was born, who lived 1/2 as long as his father. The father died 4 years after the son. How long did the father live?
Let: x = father's age at death
Equation: x/6 + x/12 + x/7 + 5 + x/2 + 4 = x
Solve: (3/28)x = 9 → x = 84
Answer: 84 years
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0212 [Fractions]
Problem: A gas tank is 1/2 full. Adding 1 gallons fills it to 2/3. How much does the tank hold?

Equation: (1/2)c + 1 = (2/3)c
Solve: c/6 = 1 → c = 1 × 6 = 6
Answer: 6

#0224 [Geometry (Pythagorean)]
Problem: A rectangular field is 4 m long. A diagonal path across it is 5 m long. How wide is the field?
Let: x = width
Equation: x² + 4² = 5²
Solve: x² − 9 = 0 → x = 3 (the other root, -3, is rejected)
Answer: 3 m
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0225 [Geometry (Pythagorean)]
Problem: A 10 ft ladder leans against a wall with its foot 6 ft from the wall. How high up the wall does it reach?
Let: h = height
Equation: 6² + h² = 10²
Solve: h² − 64 = 0 → h = 8 (the other root, -8, is rejected)
Answer: 8 ft
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0226 [Geometry (Pythagorean)]
Problem: One leg of a right triangle is 23 cm longer than the other. The hypotenuse is 37 cm. Find the legs.
Let: x = shorter leg
Equation: x² + (x + 23)² = 37²
Solve: x² + 23x − 420 = 0 → x = 12 (the other root, -35, is rejected)
Answer: 12 and 35
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0238 [Geometry (angles)]
Problem: Where two lines cross, opposite angles are (2x + 4)° and (5x − 110)°. Find the angle.
Let: x = unknown
Equation: 2x + 4 = 5x − 110
Solve: 3x = 114 → x = 38
Answer: x = 38; each angle is 80°
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0239 [Geometry (angles)]
Problem: Two parallel lines are cut by a transversal. A pair of alternate exterior angles measure (2x + 24)° and (1x + 49)°. Find x and the angle.
Let: x = unknown
Equation: 2x + 24 = x + 49
Solve: Collect like terms → x = 25
Answer: x = 25; each angle is 74°
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0240 [Geometry (angles)]
Problem: Two angles are complementary. One angle is 12 less than 5 times the other. Find both angles.
Let: x = smaller angle
Equation: x + (5x − 12) = 90
Solve: 6x = 102 → x = 17
Answer: 17° and 73°
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0241 [Geometry (angles)]

Solve: 5x = 110 → x = 22
Answer: x = 22; the angles are 117° and 63°

#0242 [Geometry (angles)]

Equation: x + x + (x + 18) = 180
Solve: 3x = 162 → x = 54
Answer: 54°, 54°, 72°

Problem: Two vertical angles measure (6x − 13)° and (4x + 17)°. Find x and the angle measure.
Let: x = unknown
Equation: 6x − 13 = 4x + 17
Solve: 2x = 30 → x = 15
Answer: x = 15; each angle is 77°
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0254 [Geometry (angles)]
Problem: Angles A and B lie on a straight line. A = (1x + 2)° and B = (3x + 138)°. What are A and B?
Let: x = unknown
Equation: (x + 2) + (3x + 138) = 180
Solve: 4x = 40 → x = 10
Answer: x = 10; the angles are 12° and 168°
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0255 [Geometry (angles)]
Problem: In a triangle, the second angle is 2 times the first, and the third angle is 108 more than the first. Find all three angles.
Let: x = first angle
Equation: x + 2x + (x + 108) = 180
Solve: 4x + 108 = 180 → x = 18
Answer: 18°, 36°, 126°
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0256 [Geometry (angles)]
Problem: In an isosceles triangle, the vertex angle is 12° less than each base angle. Find all three angles.
Let: x = each base angle
Equation: x + x + (x − 12) = 180
Solve: 3x = 192 → x = 64
Answer: 64°, 64°, 52°
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0257 [Geometry (angles)]
Problem: Two parallel lines are cut by a transversal. A pair of corresponding angles measure (4x + 4)° and (6x − 54)°. Find x and the angle.
Let: x = unknown
Equation: 4x + 4 = 6x − 54
Solve: 2x = 58 → x = 29
Answer: x = 29; each angle is 120°
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0258 [Geometry (angles)]
Problem: The angles of a triangle are in the ratio 2:6:7. Find each angle.
Let: x = scale factor
Equation: 2x + 6x + 7x = 180
Solve: 15x = 180 → x = 12
Answer: 24°, 72°, 84°
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0259 [Geometry (angles)]
Problem: Two angles are complementary. One angle is 15 more than 4 times the other. Find both angles.
Let: x = smaller angle
Equation: x + (4x + 15) = 90
Solve: 5x = 75 → x = 15
Answer: 15° and 75°
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0260 [Geometry (angles)]
Problem: An exterior angle of a triangle measures (3x + 4)°. The two remote interior angles measure (1x + 15)° and (3x − 34)°. Find x and the exterior angle.
Let: x = unknown
Equation: (x + 15) + (3x − 34) = 3x + 4
Solve: Collect like terms → x = 23
Answer: x = 23; exterior angle = 73°
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0261 [Geometry (angles)]
Problem: An exterior angle of a triangle measures (7x + 38)°. The two remote interior angles measure (4x + 15)° and (5x − 3)°. Find x and the exterior angle.
Let: x = unknown
Equation: (4x + 15) + (5x − 3) = 7x + 38
Solve: 2x = 26 → x = 13
Answer: x = 13; exterior angle = 129°
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0262 [Geometry (angles)]
Problem: Two angles form a linear pair. One measures (3x + 18)° and the other (4x − 69)°. Find both angles.
Let: x = unknown
Equation: (3x + 18) + (4x − 69) = 180
Solve: 7x = 231 → x = 33
Answer: x = 33; the angles are 117° and 63°

Answer: 31° and 149°

#0273 [Geometry (angles)]

#0274 [Geometry (area)]
Problem: If each side of a square is increased by 2 cm, its area increases by 92 cm². Find the original side length.
Let: s = original side
Equation: (s + 2)² − s² = 92
Solve: 4s = 88 → s = 22
Answer: 22 cm
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0275 [Geometry (area)]
Problem: A rectangle's length is twice its width. If the length is decreased by 6 m and the width increased by 4 m, the area stays the same. Find the original dimensions.
Let: w = width
Equation: (2w − 6)(w + 4) = 2w²
Solve: 2w = 24 → w = 12
Answer: Width 12 m, length 24 m
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0276 [Geometry (area)]
Problem: A trapezoid has parallel sides of 7 cm and 29 cm and an area of 270 cm². Find its height.
Let: h = height
Equation: (1/2)(7 + 29)h = 270
Solve: 18h = 270 → h = 15
Answer: 15 cm
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0277 [Geometry (area)]
Problem: A triangle has an area of 60 m² and a height of 3 m. How long is its base?
Let: b = base
Equation: (1/2)b(3) = 60
Solve: 1.5b = 60 → b = 40
Answer: 40 m
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0293 [Geometry (perimeter)]
Problem: A window is a rectangle 17 ft tall topped by an equilateral triangle. The triangle's sides equal the rectangle's width. The outside perimeter is 91 ft. How wide is the window?
Let: w = width
Equation: w + 2(17) + 2w = 91
Solve: 3w = 57 → w = 19
Answer: 19 ft
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0300 [Geometry (polygons)]
Problem: Each interior angle of a regular polygon measures 150°. How many sides does the polygon have?
Let: n = number of sides
Equation: 180(n − 2)/n = 150
Solve: Multiply by n: 180n − 360 = 150n → n = 12
Answer: 12 sides
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0301 [Geometry (polygons)]
Problem: The interior angles of a polygon add up to 720°. How many sides does it have?
Let: n = number of sides
Equation: 180(n − 2) = 720
Solve: 180n = 1080 → n = 6
Answer: 6 sides
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0309 [Geometry (quadratic)]
Problem: A rectangle has a perimeter of 40 ft and an area of 36 ft². Find its length and width.
Let: w = width; length = 20 − w
Equation: w(20 − w) = 36
Solve: w² − 20w + 36 = 0 → w = 2 (the other root, 18, just swaps length and width)
Answer: 18 ft by 2 ft
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0310 [Geometry (quadratic)]
Problem: A farmer has 48 m of fencing to build a rectangular pen against a barn wall (no fence on the wall side). The pen must have an area of 216 m². What dimensions work?
Let: w = each side perpendicular to the wall
Equation: w(48 − 2w) = 216
Solve: w² − 24w + 108 = 0 → w = 6 (the other root, 18, also works)
Answer: Two answers: 18 m × 12 m, or 6 m × 36 m
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0311 [Geometry (quadratic)]
Problem: Squares of side x are cut from each corner of a 29 cm by 21 cm sheet, and the sides are folded up to make an open box. The base of the box has area 209 cm². Find x.
Let: x = side of each cut square
Equation: (29 − 2x)(21 − 2x) = 209
Solve: x² − 25x + 100 = 0 → x = 5 (the other root, 20, is rejected)
Answer: 5 cm
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0312 [Geometry (quadratic)]
Problem: A rectangle's length is 12 m more than its width, and its area is 640 m². Find the dimensions.
Let: w = width
Equation: w(w + 12) = 640
Solve: w² + 12w − 640 = 0 → (w − 20)(w + 32) = 0 → w = 20 (reject negative)
Answer: Width 20 m, length 32 m
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0313 [Geometry (quadratic)]
Problem: A 19 ft by 19 ft garden has a path of uniform width around it. The path's area is 168 ft². How wide is the path?
Let: x = path width
Equation: (19 + 2x)(19 + 2x) − 361 = 168
Solve: 4x² + 76x − 168 = 0 → x = 2 (reject negative)
Answer: 2 ft
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0314 [Geometry (quadratic)]
Problem: The height of a triangle is 9 cm more than its base. The area is 243 cm². Find the base and height.
Let: b = base
Equation: (1/2)b(b + 9) = 243
Solve: b² + 9b − 486 = 0 → b = 18 (the other root, -27, is rejected)
Answer: Base 18 cm, height 27 cm
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0315 [Geometry (quadratic)]
Problem: A 18 in × 15 in photo is surrounded by a frame of uniform width. The framed picture covers 418 in². How wide is the frame?
Let: x = frame width
Equation: (18 + 2x)(15 + 2x) = 418
Solve: 2x² + 33x − 74 = 0 → x = 2 (the other root, -18.5, is rejected)
Answer: 2 in
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0337 [Geometry (quadratic)]
Problem: Squares of side x are cut from each corner of a 26 cm by 13 cm sheet, and the sides are folded up to make an open box. The base of the box has area 48 cm². Find x.
Let: x = side of each cut square
Equation: (26 − 2x)(13 − 2x) = 48
Solve: 2x² − 39x + 145 = 0 → x = 5 (the other root, 14.5, is rejected)
Answer: 5 cm
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0338 [Geometry (quadratic)]
Problem: The height of a triangle is 8 cm more than its base. The area is 384 cm². Find the base and height.
Let: b = base
Equation: (1/2)b(b + 8) = 384
Solve: b² + 8b − 768 = 0 → b = 24 (the other root, -32, is rejected)
Answer: Base 24 cm, height 32 cm
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0339 [Geometry (quadratic)]
Problem: A 9 in × 5 in photo is surrounded by a frame of uniform width. The framed picture covers 117 in². How wide is the frame?
Let: x = frame width
Equation: (9 + 2x)(5 + 2x) = 117
Solve: x² + 7x − 18 = 0 → x = 2 (the other root, -9, is rejected)
Answer: 2 in
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0340 [Geometry (quadratic)]
Problem: A 7 ft by 17 ft garden has a path of uniform width around it. The path's area is 112 ft². How wide is the path?
Let: x = path width
Equation: (7 + 2x)(17 + 2x) − 119 = 112
Solve: 4x² + 48x − 112 = 0 → x = 2 (reject negative)
Answer: 2 ft
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0341 [Geometry (rectangle)]
Problem: The length of a rectangle is 3 less than 3 times its width. The perimeter is 178 cm. Find the dimensions.
Let: w = width
Equation: 2w + 2(3w − 3) = 178
Solve: 8w − 6 = 178 → w = 23
Answer: Width 23 cm, length 66 cm
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0342 [Geometry (rectangle)]
Problem: The length of a rectangle is 2 more than its width. The perimeter is 132 cm. Find the dimensions.
Let: w = width
Equation: 2w + 2(w + 2) = 132
Solve: 4w + 4 = 132 → w = 32
Answer: Width 32 cm, length 34 cm
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0343 [Geometry (rectangle)]

Equation: 2w + 2(3w − 4) = 104
Solve: 8w − 8 = 104 → w = 14
Answer: Width 14 cm, length 38 cm

#0345 [Geometry (rectangle)]
Problem: The length of a rectangle is 1 less than 2 times its width. The perimeter is 166 cm. Find the dimensions.
Let: w = width
Equation: 2w + 2(2w − 1) = 166
Solve: 6w − 2 = 166 → w = 28
Answer: Width 28 cm, length 55 cm
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0346 [Geometry (triangle)]
Problem: Each of the two equal sides of an isosceles triangle is 2 cm longer than the base. The perimeter is 52 cm. Find the side lengths.
Let: x = base
Equation: x + 2(x + 2) = 52
Solve: 3x + 4 = 52 → x = 16
Answer: Base 16 cm, equal sides 18 cm
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0347 [Geometry (triangle)]
Problem: A triangle's second side is 11 in longer than the first, and its third side is 5 in less than twice the first. If the perimeter is 122 in, how long is each side?
Let: x = first side
Equation: x + (x + 11) + (2x − 5) = 122
Solve: 4x = 116 → x = 29
Answer: 29 in, 40 in, 53 in
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0348 [Geometry (triangle)]
Problem: A triangle's second side is 4 in longer than the first, and its third side is 5 in less than twice the first. If the perimeter is 47 in, how long is each side?
Let: x = first side
Equation: x + (x + 4) + (2x − 5) = 47
Solve: 4x = 48 → x = 12
Answer: 12 in, 16 in, 19 in

#0355 [Geometry (volume)]
Problem: A rectangular box is 16 in long and 5 in wide. Its volume is 320 in³. How tall is it?
Let: h = height
Equation: 16 × 5 × h = 320
Solve: 80h = 320 → h = 4
Answer: 4 in
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0356 [Geometry (volume)]

Let: h = height
Equation: 16 × 4 × h = 384
Solve: 64h = 384 → h = 6
Answer: 6 dm

#0359 [Inequality]
Problem: A school dance costs $303 to run. Tickets are $8. How many tickets must be sold for a profit of at least $2200?
Let: n = tickets
Equation: 8n − 303 ≥ 2200
Solve: n ≥ 312.875 → n = 313 (smallest whole number that works)
Answer: 313 tickets

#0360 [Inequality]
Problem: An elevator holds at most 1200 lb. A 200 lb worker is loading 60 lb boxes. How many boxes can go up with the worker in one trip?
Let: b = boxes
Equation: 200 + 60b ≤ 1200
Solve: b ≤ 50/3 → b = 16 (largest whole number that works)
Answer: 16 boxes

#0361 [Inequality]
Problem: The temperature is 26°F and falling 3°F per hour. For how many whole hours will it stay at or above 1°F?
Let: h = hours
Equation: 26 − 3h ≥ 1
Solve: h ≤ 25/3 → h = 8 (largest whole number that works)
Answer: 8 hours

#0401 [Inverse variation]
Problem: 12 workers can build a wall in 10 days. At the same rate, how many days would 3 workers take?
Let: d = days
Equation: 12 × 10 = 3 × d
Solve: d = 120/3 = 40
Answer: 40 days
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0402 [Inverse variation]
Problem: A gear with 24 teeth turning at 40 rpm drives a gear with 20 teeth. Teeth × rpm is the same for both gears. How fast does the second gear turn?
Let: r = rpm
Equation: 24 × 40 = 20r
Solve: 20r = 960 → r = 48
Answer: 48 rpm
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0403 [Inverse variation]
Problem: At 72 km/h a trip takes 5 hours. Time varies inversely with speed. How long would it take at 90 km/h?
Let: t = hours
Equation: 72 × 5 = 90t
Solve: 90t = 360 → t = 4
Answer: 4 hours
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0404 [Inverse variation]
Problem: A gear with 24 teeth turning at 80 rpm drives a gear with 15 teeth. Teeth × rpm is the same for both gears. How fast does the second gear turn?
Let: r = rpm
Equation: 24 × 80 = 15r
Solve: 15r = 1920 → r = 128
Answer: 128 rpm
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0415 [Joint variation]
Problem: 6 workers working 10 hours a day finish a job in 5 days. How many days would 4 workers working 5 hours a day take?
Let: d = days
Equation: 6 × 10 × 5 = 4 × 5 × d
Solve: 20d = 300 → d = 15
Answer: 15 days
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0416 [Joint variation]
Problem: 6 workers working 12 hours a day finish a job in 6 days. How many days would 4 workers working 6 hours a day take?
Let: d = days
Equation: 6 × 12 × 6 = 4 × 6 × d
Solve: 24d = 432 → d = 18
Answer: 18 days
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0419 [Joint variation]
Problem: 3 workers working 6 hours a day finish a job in 6 days. How many days would 2 workers working 9 hours a day take?

Equation: 3 × 6 × 6 = 2 × 9 × d
Solve: 18d = 108 → d = 6
Answer: 6 days

#0420 [Linear cost]
Problem: A venue charges $100 plus $15 per guest. The total was $865. How many guests came?
Let: n = guests
Equation: 100 + 15n = 865
Solve: 15n = 765 → n = 51
Answer: 51 guests
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0421 [Linear cost]
Problem: A gym charges a $50 joining fee and $30 per month. Carla has paid $470 in total. How many months has Carla been a member?
Let: n = months
Equation: 50 + 30n = 470
Solve: 30n = 420 → n = 14
Answer: 14 months
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0422 [Linear cost]
Problem: Yara works two jobs paying $16/h and $22/h. Last month 61 hours were worked in total, earning $1156. How many hours were worked at the $16 job?
Let: h = hours at $16
Equation: 16h + 22(61 − h) = 1156
Solve: 6h = 186 → h = 31
Answer: 31 hours at $16, 30 hours at $22
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0423 [Linear cost]
Problem: A phone plan costs $50 a month and includes 5 GB of data. Each extra GB costs $5. The bill was $90. How much data was used?
Let: g = GB used
Equation: 50 + 5(g − 5) = 90
Solve: 5g = 65 → g = 13
Answer: 13 GB
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0424 [Linear cost]
Problem: Umar gets $10 allowance a week plus $4 for each extra chore. Over 8 weeks that came to $120. How many extra chores were done?
Let: n = chores
Equation: 10(8) + 4n = 120
Solve: 4n = 40 → n = 10
Answer: 10 chores
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0425 [Linear cost]
Problem: Notebooks cost $3.50 each. With a $3 coupon, Hiro paid $46.00. How many notebooks were bought?
Let: n = items
Equation: 3.5n − 3 = 46

#0480 [Linear model]
Problem: Town A has 18,000 people and grows by 2000 a year. Town B has 63,000 people and shrinks by 500 a year. In how many years will they be equal?

Answer: 18 years

Answer: 176 minutes

#0482 [Linear model]
Problem: For a taxi ride, 4 miles cost $46 and 11 miles cost $88. The cost is linear: C = m·x + b. Find m and b, then how many miles cost $136.
Let: m = rate, b = fixed fee
Equation: 4m + b = 46;  11m + b = 88
Solve: Subtract the equations → m = 6, b = 22
Answer: m = 6, b = 22; 19 miles
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Solve: 2w = 15 → w = 7.5
Answer: 7.5 weeks

#0516 [Linear model]
Problem: One candle is 30 cm tall and burns 1.5 cm per hour. Another is 36 cm tall and burns 3.5 cm per hour. When will they be the same height?
Let: t = hours
Equation: 30 − 1.5t = 36 − 3.5t
Solve: 2t = 6 → t = 3
Answer: 3 hours
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0517 [Linear model]
Problem: A club has raised $260 so far. At its bake sale it charges $12 per box of cookies. How many more boxes must it sell to reach its $344 goal?
Let: n = sales
Equation: 260 + 12n = 344
Solve: 12n = 84 → n = 7
Answer: 7
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0518 [Linear model]
Problem: Town A has 32,000 people and grows by 2600 a year. Town B has 70,000 people and shrinks by 1200 a year. In how many years will they be equal?
Let: y = years
Equation: 32000 + 2600y = 70000 − 1200y
Solve: 3800y = 38000 → y = 10
Answer: 10 years
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0519 [Linear model]
Problem: A pool holds 9,100 L and drains at 370 L per minute. After how many minutes will 1,700 L remain?
Let: t = minutes
Equation: 9100 − 370t = 1700
Solve: 370t = 7400 → t = 20
Answer: 20 minutes
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0520 [Linear model]
Problem: Plant A is 15 cm tall and grows 3 cm a week. Plant B is 35 cm tall and grows 2 cm a week. After how many weeks will they be the same height?
Let: w = weeks
Equation: 15 + 3w = 35 + 2w
Solve: Collect like terms → w = 20
Answer: 20 weeks
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0521 [Linear model]
Problem: Sound travels about 343 m/s, so d = 343t. A storm is 1029 m away. How many seconds after the lightning flash will the thunder be heard?
Let: t = seconds
Equation: 343t = 1029
Solve: 343t = 1029 → t = 3
Answer: 3 seconds
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0522 [Linear model]
Problem: For a plumber's bill, 5 hours cost $107 and 7 hours cost $121. The cost is linear: C = m·x + b. Find m and b, then how many hours cost $142.
Let: m = rate, b = fixed fee
Equation: 5m + b = 107;  7m + b = 121
Solve: Subtract the equations → m = 7, b = 72
Answer: m = 7, b = 72; 10 hours
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0523 [Linear model]
Problem: A phone at 95% battery loses 6% per hour. After how many hours will it be at 20%?
Let: h = hours
Equation: 95 − 6h = 20
Solve: 6h = 75 → h = 12.5
Answer: 12.5 hours
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0524 [Linear model]
Problem: A phone at 100% battery loses 5% per hour. After how many hours will it be at 20%?
Let: h = hours
Equation: 100 − 5h = 20
Solve: 5h = 80 → h = 16
Answer: 16 hours
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0525 [Linear model]
Problem: A 102-page book has 32 pages already read. How many pages a day must be read to finish in 5 days?
Let: p = pages per day
Equation: 32 + 5p = 102
Solve: 5p = 70 → p = 14
Answer: 14 pages per day

#0529 [Linear model]
Problem: One candle is 11 cm tall and burns 1 cm per hour. Another is 14 cm tall and burns 2 cm per hour. When will they be the same height?
Let: t = hours
Equation: 11 − t = 14 − 2t
Solve: Collect like terms → t = 3
Answer: 3 hours

#0530 [Linear model]
Problem: For a plumber's bill, 1 hours cost $66 and 9 hours cost $178. The cost is linear: C = m·x + b. Find m and b, then how many hours cost $234.
Let: m = rate, b = fixed fee
Equation: m + b = 66;  9m + b = 178
Solve: Subtract the equations → m = 14, b = 52
Answer: m = 14, b = 52; 13 hours

#0531 [Linear model]
Problem: Town A has 34,000 people and grows by 500 a year. Town B has 67,000 people and shrinks by 1000 a year. In how many years will they be equal?
Let: y = years
Equation: 34000 + 500y = 67000 − 1000y
Solve: 1500y = 33000 → y = 22
Answer: 22 years
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0532 [Mixture]
Problem: How many pounds of coffee worth $10/lb must be added to 14 lb of coffee worth $8/lb to make a mix worth $9/lb?
Let: x = pounds added
Equation: 8(14) + 10x = 9(14 + x)
Solve: Collect like terms → x = 14
Answer: 14 lb
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0533 [Mixture]
Problem: How many liters of a 25% juice drink must be mixed with a 50% juice drink to make 100 liters of a 39% juice drink?
Let: x = liters of 25%; 100 − x = liters of 50%
Equation: 0.25x + 0.5(100 − x) = 0.39(100)
Solve: 0.25x = 11 → x = 44
Answer: 44 L of the 25% and 56 L of the 50%
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0534 [Mixture]
Problem: One alloy is 25% nickel and another is 75% nickel. How many kilograms of each are needed to make 25 kg of an alloy that is 31% nickel?
Let: x = kg of the 25% alloy
Equation: 0.25x + 0.75(25 − x) = 0.31(25)
Solve: x/2 = 11 → x = 11 × 2 = 22
Answer: 22 kg of 25%, 3 kg of 75%
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0535 [Mixture]
Problem: How much water must be added to 4 L of a 30% salt solution to make it 5% salt?
Let: w = liters of water
Equation: 0.3(4) = 0.05(4 + w)
Solve: w/20 = 1 → w = 1 × 20 = 20
Answer: 20 L
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0536 [Mixture]
Problem: A 30 L tank of 6% salt water is left to evaporate until it is 15% salt. How much water evaporates?
Let: e = liters evaporated
Equation: 0.06(30) = 0.15(30 − e)
Solve: 0.15e = 2.7 → e = 18
Answer: 18 L
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0537 [Mixture]
Problem: 50 lb of a blend of coffee beans selling for $13.60/lb is made from coffee beans costing $8/lb and $15/lb. How many pounds of each are used?
Let: x = lb at $8; 50 − x = lb at $15
Equation: 8x + 15(50 − x) = 680
Solve: 7x = 70 → x = 10
Answer: 10 lb at $8 and 40 lb at $15
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0538 [Mixture]
Problem: How many liters of pure antifreeze must be added to 4 L of a 30% antifreeze mix to get a 80% mix?
Let: x = liters of pure antifreeze
Equation: 0.3(4) + x = 0.8(4 + x)
Solve: x/5 = 2 → x = 2 × 5 = 10
Answer: 10 L
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0539 [Mixture]
Problem: A 20-liter radiator holds a 50% antifreeze mix. How much must be drained and replaced with pure antifreeze to raise it to 70%?
Let: x = liters replaced
Equation: 0.5(20 − x) + x = 0.7(20)
Solve: x/2 = 4 → x = 4 × 2 = 8
Answer: 8 L
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0570 [Number puzzle]
Problem: 6 times the difference of a number and 6 is 174. What is the number?
Let: x = the number
Equation: 6(x − 6) = 174
Solve: 6x = 210 → x = 35
Answer: 35

#0571 [Number puzzle]
Problem: The second of three numbers is 3 times the first. The third is 8 more than the second. Their sum is 176. Find the numbers.
Let: x = first number
Equation: x + 3x + (3x + 8) = 176
Solve: 7x = 168 → x = 24
Answer: 24, 72, 80

Solve: 5x = 35 → x = 7
Answer: Aiden: 7, Tara: 31

Answer: -28

Problem: Four times a number, increased by 1, is 117. Find the number.
Let: x = the number
Equation: 4x + 1 = 117
Solve: 4x = 116 → x = 29
Answer: 29
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0605 [Number puzzle]
Problem: The second of three numbers is 4 times the first. The third is 1 more than the second. Their sum is 109. Find the numbers.
Let: x = first number
Equation: x + 4x + (4x + 1) = 109
Solve: 9x = 108 → x = 12
Answer: 12, 48, 49
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0606 [Number puzzle]
Problem: 7 times a number minus 16 equals 2 times the number plus 34. Find the number.
Let: x = the number
Equation: 7x − 16 = 2x + 34
Solve: 5x = 50 → x = 10
Answer: 10
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0607 [Number puzzle]
Problem: Victor has 2 times as much money as Yara. After Victor gives Yara $25, they have the same amount. How much did each have at first?
Let: x = Yara's money
Equation: 2x − 25 = x + 25
Solve: Collect like terms → x = 50
Answer: Yara $50, Victor $100
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0608 [Number puzzle]
Problem: 6 times the difference of a number and 11 is -108. What is the number?
Let: x = the number
Equation: 6(x − 11) = -108
Solve: 6x = -42 → x = -7
Answer: -7
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0609 [Number puzzle]
Problem: 4/5 of a number plus 20 is 44. Find the number.
Let: x = the number
Equation: (4/5)x + 20 = 44
Solve: (4/5)x = 24 → x = 30
Answer: 30
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Solve: 9x = 216 → x = 24
Answer: 24

#0611 [Number puzzle]
Problem: Maya has 5 more than 4 times as many stamps as Ben. Together they have 285 stamps. How many does each have?
Let: x = Ben's stamps

Solve: 5x = 280 → x = 56
Answer: Ben: 56, Maya: 229

Answer: $8,000.00

#0686 [Profit]
Problem: A candle shop has fixed costs of $2000. Each item costs $2 to make and sells for $17. How many items must be sold to make a profit of $9775?
Let: n = items
Equation: 17n − (2000 + 2n) = 9775
Solve: 15n = 11775 → n = 785
Answer: 785 items
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0691 [Proportion]
Problem: A model car is built at a 1:50 scale. The real car is 50 cm long. How long is the model?
Let: x = model length
Equation: 1/50 = x/50
Solve: x/50 = 0.02 → x = 0.02 × 50 = 1
Answer: 1
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0692 [Proportion]
Problem: A lawn needs 4 kg of fertilizer per 25 m². How much is needed for 400 m²?
Let: x = kg needed
Equation: 4/25 = x/400
Solve: x/400 = 0.16 → x = 0.16 × 400 = 64
Answer: 64 kg
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0693 [Proportion]
Problem: A shade of green uses blue and yellow paint in the ratio 2:3. If 6 L of blue is used, how much yellow is needed?
Let: y = liters of yellow
Equation: 2/3 = 6/y
Solve: Cross-multiply → y = 9
Answer: 9 L
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0694 [Proportion]
Problem: A recipe uses 0.5 cups of flour for 4 servings. How many cups are needed for 8 servings?
Let: x = cups needed
Equation: 0.5/4 = x/8
Solve: x = 0.5 × 8 / 4 = 1
Answer: 1 cup
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0695 [Proportion]

Equation: 1.25d = 1875
Solve: 1.25d = 1875 → d = 1500
Answer: $1,500.00
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Answer: 10 eggs

Answer: 19 cm

----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0701 [Proportion]
Problem: A shade of green uses blue and yellow paint in the ratio 2:4. If 10 L of blue is used, how much yellow is needed?
Let: y = liters of yellow
Equation: 2/4 = 10/y
Solve: Cross-multiply → y = 20
Answer: 20 L
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0702 [Proportion]
Problem: A recipe uses 0.5 cups of flour for 2 servings. How many cups are needed for 5 servings?
Let: x = cups needed
Equation: 0.5/2 = x/5
Solve: x = 0.5 × 5 / 2 = 1.25
Answer: 1.25 cups
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0703 [Proportion]
Problem: Suppose 1 dollar buys 1.35 units of a foreign currency. How many dollars do you need to get 1188 units?
Let: d = dollars
Equation: 1.35d = 1188
Solve: 1.35d = 1188 → d = 880
Answer: $880.00
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0704 [Proportion]
Problem: A model car is built at a 1:48 scale. The real car is 768 cm long. How long is the model?
Let: x = model length
Equation: 1/48 = x/768
Solve: x/768 = 1/48 → x = 1/48 × 768 = 16
Answer: 16
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0705 [Proportion]
Problem: Suppose 1 dollar buys 110 units of a foreign currency. How many dollars do you need to get 6600 units?
Let: d = dollars
Equation: 110d = 6600
Solve: 110d = 6600 → d = 60
Answer: $60.00
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0706 [Proportion]

Equation: 5/20 = x/120
Solve: x/120 = 0.25 → x = 0.25 × 120 = 30
Answer: 30 kg

#0709 [Proportion]
Problem: On a map, 1 cm represents 25 km. Two cities are 237.5 km apart. How far apart are they on the map?
Let: x = map distance in cm
Equation: 1/25 = x/237.5
Solve: x = 237.5/25 = 9.5
Answer: 9.5 cm

#0725 [Proportion (similar triangles)]
Problem: A 5 ft person casts a 2 ft shadow. At the same time a tree casts a 30 ft shadow. How tall is the tree?
Let: h = height
Equation: 5/2 = h/30
Solve: h/30 = 2.5 → h = 2.5 × 30 = 75
Answer: 75
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0726 [Proportion (similar triangles)]
Problem: At noon a 6 m post casts a 10 m shadow, and a building nearby casts a 150 m shadow. How tall is the building?
Let: h = height
Equation: 6/10 = h/150
Solve: h/150 = 0.6 → h = 0.6 × 150 = 90
Answer: 90
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Answer: 28

#0729 [Quadratic]
Problem: The square of a positive number is 48 more than 2 times the number. Find the number.
Let: x = the number
Equation: x² = 2x + 48
Solve: x² − 2x − 48 = 0 → x = 8 (the other root, -6, is rejected)
Answer: 8
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0730 [Quadratic]
Problem: The sum of the squares of two consecutive positive integers is 85. Find the integers.
Let: x = smaller integer
Equation: x² + (x + 1)² = 85
Solve: x² + x − 42 = 0 → x = 6 (the other root, -7, is rejected)
Answer: 6 and 7
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0731 [Quadratic]
Problem: The square of a positive number plus 16 times the number equals 720. Find the number.
Let: x = the number
Equation: x² + 16x = 720
Solve: x² + 16x − 720 = 0 → (x − 20)(x + 36) = 0 → x = 20
Answer: 20
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0732 [Quadratic]
Problem: A polygon has 5 diagonals. Using D = n(n − 3)/2, how many sides does it have?
Let: n = number of sides
Equation: n(n − 3)/2 = 5
Solve: n² − 3n − 10 = 0 → n = 5 (the other root, -2, is rejected)
Answer: 5 sides
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0733 [Quadratic]
Problem: The product of two consecutive positive integers is 12. Find the integers.
Let: x = smaller integer
Equation: x(x + 1) = 12
Solve: x² + x − 12 = 0 → (x − 3)(x + 4) = 0 → x = 3
Answer: 3 and 4
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0734 [Quadratic]
Problem: Two numbers add up to 56 and multiply to 780. What are they?
Let: x = one number; other = 56 − x
Equation: x(56 − x) = 780
Solve: x² − 56x + 780 = 0 → x = 26 (the other root, 30, gives the same pair)
Answer: 26 and 30
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0755 [Quadratic (counting)]
Problem: At a party everyone shook hands with everyone else exactly once, for 10 handshakes. How many people were there?
Let: n = number of people/teams
Equation: n(n − 1)/2 = 10
Solve: n² − n − 20 = 0 → n = 5 (the other root, -4, is rejected)
Answer: 5
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0756 [Quadratic (counting)]

Let: n = number of people/teams
Equation: n(n − 1)/2 = 561
Solve: n² − n − 1122 = 0 → n = 34 (the other root, -33, is rejected)
Answer: 34
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0757 [Quadratic (counting)]
Problem: In a round-robin tournament each team plays every other team once, for 45 games. How many teams are there?
Let: n = number of people/teams
Equation: n(n − 1)/2 = 45
Solve: n² − n − 90 = 0 → n = 10 (the other root, -9, is rejected)
Answer: 10
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0759 [Quadratic (projectile)]
Problem: A rocket is launched upward from a height of 96 ft at 16 ft/s. Its height is h = −16t² + 16t + 96. After how many seconds does it hit the ground?
Let: t = seconds
Equation: −16t² + 16t + 96 = 0
Solve: Factor/quadratic formula → t = 3 (other root t = -2 rejected)
Answer: 3 seconds
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0764 [Quadratic (sequences)]
Problem: How many consecutive whole numbers, starting from 1, must be added to get a sum of 1326? (Use 1 + 2 + … + n = n(n + 1)/2.)
Let: n = how many numbers
Equation: n(n + 1)/2 = 1326
Solve: n² + n − 2652 = 0 → n = 51 (the other root, -52, is rejected)
Answer: 51 numbers (1 through 51)
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0765 [Quadratic (sequences)]
Problem: A stack of cans has 1 can in the top row, and each row below has 1 more than the row above. The stack has 15 cans in total. How many rows are there?
Let: n = number of rows
Equation: (n/2)(2(1) + (n − 1)(1)) = 15
Solve: n² + n − 30 = 0 → n = 5 (the other root, -6, is rejected)
Answer: 5 rows
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0766 [Quadratic (sequences)]
Problem: A stack of cans has 8 cans in the top row, and each row below has 2 more than the row above. The stack has 260 cans in total. How many rows are there?
Let: n = number of rows
Equation: (n/2)(2(8) + (n − 1)(2)) = 260
Solve: n² + 7n − 260 = 0 → n = 13 (the other root, -20, is rejected)
Answer: 13 rows
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0774 [Rate]
Problem: A car travels 364 miles on 13 gallons of gas. At this rate, how many gallons are needed for 392 miles?
Let: x = gallons
Equation: 364/13 = 392/x
Solve: 28x = 392 → x = 14
Answer: 14 gallons
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0775 [Rate]
Problem: A car travels 475 miles on 19 gallons of gas. At this rate, how many gallons are needed for 625 miles?
Let: x = gallons
Equation: 475/19 = 625/x
Solve: 25x = 625 → x = 25
Answer: 25 gallons
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0779 [Ratio]
Problem: The ratio of boys to girls in a class is 3:8. After 4 more girls join, the ratio becomes 1:3. How many students were there originally?
Let: k = scale factor (boys = 3k, girls = 8k)
Equation: 3k/(8k + 4) = 1/3
Solve: Multiply both sides by the denominator and collect terms → k = 4
Answer: 44 students (12 boys, 32 girls)
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0780 [Ratio]
Problem: An estate of $64,500.00 is split among three heirs. Wes gets 2 times as much as Nia, and Noah gets $6,500.00 more than Nia. How much does each receive?
Let: x = Nia's share
Equation: 2x + x + (x + 6500) = 64500
Solve: 4x = 58000 → x = 14500
Answer: Wes $29,000.00, Nia $14,500.00, Noah $21,000.00
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0781 [Ratio]
Problem: A mix uses cement, sand, and gravel in the ratio 8:4:5. The total is 204 kg. How much of each is used?
Let: k = one part
Equation: 8k + 4k + 5k = 204
Solve: 17k = 204 → k = 12
Answer: 96, 48, 60 kg
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0782 [Ratio]
Problem: The ratio of Yusuf's marbles to Dev's is 3:5. After Yusuf gives Dev 12 marbles, the ratio is 1:7. How many did each have at first?
Let: k = scale factor
Equation: (3k − 12)/(5k + 12) = 1/7
Solve: Multiply both sides by the denominator and collect terms → k = 6
Answer: Yusuf 18, Dev 30
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0783 [Ratio]
Problem: Sofia and Pablo invested in a business in the ratio 2:1. They share a profit of $22,500.00 in the same ratio. How much does each get?
Let: k = one share
Equation: 2k + k = 22500
Solve: 3k = 22500 → k = 7500
Answer: Sofia $15,000.00, Pablo $7,500.00
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0784 [Ratio]
Problem: A 10 m rope is cut into two pieces in the ratio 1:4. How long is each piece?
Let: x = scale factor
Equation: x + 4x = 10
Solve: 5x = 10 → x = 2
Answer: 2 m and 8 m
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0785 [Ratio]
Problem: A 105 cm board is cut into two pieces. One piece is 7 cm longer than the other. How long is each piece?
Let: x = shorter piece
Equation: x + (x + 7) = 105
Solve: 2x = 98 → x = 49
Answer: 49 cm and 56 cm
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0787 [Ratio]
Problem: The ratio of Hana's marbles to Maya's is 1:1. After Hana gives Maya 23 marbles, the ratio is 1:3. How many did each have at first?
Let: k = scale factor
Equation: (k − 23)/(k + 23) = 1/3
Solve: Multiply both sides by the denominator and collect terms → k = 46
Answer: Hana 46, Maya 46
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0788 [Ratio]
Problem: A 42 m rope is cut into two pieces in the ratio 5:1. How long is each piece?
Let: x = scale factor
Equation: 5x + x = 42
Solve: 6x = 42 → x = 7
Answer: 35 m and 7 m
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0789 [Ratio]
Problem: The ratio of boys to girls in a class is 6:3. After 6 more girls join, the ratio becomes 8:5. How many students were there originally?
Let: k = scale factor (boys = 6k, girls = 3k)
Equation: 6k/(3k + 6) = 8/5
Solve: Multiply both sides by the denominator and collect terms → k = 8
Answer: 72 students (48 boys, 24 girls)
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0790 [Ratio]
Problem: An estate of $92,000.00 is split among three heirs. Finn gets 3 times as much as Sam, and Ben gets $2,000.00 more than Sam. How much does each receive?
Let: x = Sam's share
Equation: 3x + x + (x + 2000) = 92000
Solve: 5x = 90000 → x = 18000
Answer: Finn $54,000.00, Sam $18,000.00, Ben $20,000.00
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0791 [Ratio]
Problem: A 92 cm board is cut into two pieces. One piece is 26 cm longer than the other. How long is each piece?
Let: x = shorter piece
Equation: x + (x + 26) = 92
Solve: 2x = 66 → x = 33
Answer: 33 cm and 59 cm

#0812 [Rational equation]
Problem: A field trip costs $350 for the bus plus $15 admission per student. The bus cost is shared equally, and each student pays $32.50 in total. How many students went?
Let: n = students
Equation: 350/n + 15 = 32.5
Solve: Subtract 15, then n = 350 ÷ 17.5 → n = 20
Answer: 20 students
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0813 [Rational equation]
Problem: A plane flies 1590 miles with a 40 mph tailwind in the same time it flies 1350 miles into the same wind as a headwind. What is the plane's speed in still air?
Let: p = plane speed
Equation: 1590/(p + 40) = 1350/(p − 40)
Solve: Cross-multiply → p = 490
Answer: 490 mph
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0814 [Rational equation]
Problem: The denominator of a fraction is 2 more than the numerator. If 4 is added to both, the fraction becomes 7/8. Find the original fraction.
Let: x = numerator
Equation: (x + 4)/(x + 6) = 7/8
Solve: Cross-multiply and solve → x = 10
Answer: 10/12
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0815 [Rational equation]
Problem: A number plus its reciprocal equals 2.5. Find the number.
Let: x = the number
Equation: x + 1/x = 2.5
Solve: Multiply by x: x² − (2.5)x + 1 = 0 → x = 2
Answer: 2 or 1/2
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0816 [Rational equation]
Problem: A group shares a $42 rental equally. If 2 more people join, each pays $5.60 less. How many people are in the group originally?
Let: n = original number of people
Equation: 42/n − 42/(n + 2) = 5.6
Solve: Multiply out → quadratic in n → n = 3 (reject negative)
Answer: 3 people
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0817 [Rational equation]
Problem: A truck travels 96 miles in the same time a car travels 138 miles. The car is 14 mph faster. Find both speeds.
Let: s = truck speed
Equation: 96/s = 138/(s + 14)
Solve: Cross-multiply: 96(s + 14) = 138s → s = 32
Answer: Truck 32 mph, car 46 mph
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0818 [Rational equation]
Problem: A boat goes 52 km upstream in the same time it goes 132 km downstream. Its speed in still water is 23 km/h. What is the speed of the current?
Let: c = current speed
Equation: 52/(23 − c) = 132/(23 + c)
Solve: Cross-multiply → c = 10
Answer: 10 km/h
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Let: d = distance
Equation: d/10 − d/24 = 2.8
Solve: Multiply by 120 → d = 48
Answer: 48 km

#0848 [Savings]
Problem: Ben has $150 saved and adds $25 each week. Ava has $310 saved and adds $15 each week. After how many weeks will they have the same amount?

Equation: 150 + 25w = 310 + 15w
Solve: 10w = 160 → w = 16
Answer: 16 weeks (each will have $550)

#0857 [Sequences]
Problem: A theater has 29 seats in the first row and each row has 5 more seats than the row before. Which row has 209 seats?

Equation: 29 + (n − 1)(5) = 209

Answer: Row 37

#0862 [Simple interest]
Problem: Dev has $18,000.00 invested at 3%. How much more must be invested at 9% so that the total earns 6% overall?
Let: x = extra amount
Equation: 0.03(18000) + 0.09x = 0.06(18000 + x)
Solve: 0.03x = 540 → x = 18000
Answer: $18,000.00
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0863 [Simple interest]
Problem: Quinn invests $31,000.00, part at 2% and the rest at 3% simple annual interest. After one year the total interest is $770.00. How much was invested at each rate?
Let: x = amount at 2%; 31000 − x = amount at 3%
Equation: 0.02x + 0.03(31000 − x) = 770
Solve: 0.01x = 160 → x = 16000
Answer: $16,000.00 at 2% and $15,000.00 at 3%
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0864 [Simple interest]
Problem: $16,500.00 is split between two accounts paying 6% and 4% simple interest. Both accounts earn the same interest in a year. How much is in each?
Let: x = amount at 6%
Equation: 0.06x = 0.04(16500 − x)
Solve: x/10 = 660 → x = 660 × 10 = 6600
Answer: $6,600.00 at 6%, $9,900.00 at 4%
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0865 [Simple interest]
Problem: How many years will it take $2,000.00 to earn $480.00 in simple interest at 4% per year?
Let: t = years
Equation: 2000 × 0.04 × t = 480
Solve: 80t = 480 → t = 6
Answer: 6 years
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0866 [Simple interest]

Let: t = years
Equation: 25500 × 0.04 × t = 7140
Solve: 1020t = 7140 → t = 7
Answer: 7 years
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Problem: How many years will it take $23,500.00 to earn $18,800.00 in simple interest at 8% per year?
Let: t = years
Equation: 23500 × 0.08 × t = 18800
Solve: 1880t = 18800 → t = 10
Answer: 10 years
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0871 [Simple interest]
Problem: Yusuf has $19,000.00 invested at 4%. How much more must be invested at 9% so that the total earns 7% overall?
Let: x = extra amount
Equation: 0.04(19000) + 0.09x = 0.07(19000 + x)
Solve: x/50 = 570 → x = 570 × 50 = 28500
Answer: $28,500.00
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0872 [Simple interest]
Problem: Kofi invests $30,500.00, part at 2% and the rest at 4% simple annual interest. After one year the total interest is $830.00. How much was invested at each rate?
Let: x = amount at 2%; 30500 − x = amount at 4%
Equation: 0.02x + 0.04(30500 − x) = 830
Solve: 0.02x = 390 → x = 19500
Answer: $19,500.00 at 2% and $11,000.00 at 4%
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0873 [Simple interest]
Problem: $22,500.00 is split between two accounts paying 2% and 4% simple interest. Both accounts earn the same interest in a year. How much is in each?
Let: x = amount at 2%
Equation: 0.02x = 0.04(22500 − x)
Solve: 0.06x = 900 → x = 15000
Answer: $15,000.00 at 2%, $7,500.00 at 4%
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0874 [Simple interest]
Problem: $15,000.00 earns $5,400.00 in simple interest over 9 years. What is the annual interest rate?
Let: r = rate (as a percent)
Equation: 15000 × (r/100) × 9 = 5400
Solve: 1350r = 5400 → r = 4
Answer: 4%
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0875 [Simple interest]
Problem: Dana invests $13,500.00, part at 2% and the rest at 5% simple annual interest. After one year the total interest is $450.00. How much was invested at each rate?
Let: x = amount at 2%; 13500 − x = amount at 5%
Equation: 0.02x + 0.05(13500 − x) = 450
Solve: 0.03x = 225 → x = 7500
Answer: $7,500.00 at 2% and $6,000.00 at 5%
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0885 [System of equations]
Problem: Jade is 5 times as old as Emeka. In 5 years, Jade will be 4 times as old as Emeka. How old is each now?
Let: p = Jade's age, c = Emeka's age
Equation: p = 5c;  p + 5 = 4(c + 5)
Solve: Substitute p = 5c → p = 75, c = 15
Answer: Jade 75, Emeka 15

#0889 [System of equations]
Problem: The perimeter of a rectangle is 70 m and its length is 7 m more than its width. Find the length and width.

Answer: 21 m by 14 m

Solve: Solve by elimination → x = 6, y = 16
Answer: 6 $5 bills and 16 $10 bills
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0946 [System of equations]
Problem: A banquet hall seats 86 guests at 27 tables. Some tables seat 2 and the others seat 4. How many of each kind are there?
Let: x = 2-seat tables, y = 4-seat tables
Equation: x + y = 27;  2x + 4y = 86
Solve: Solve by elimination → x = 11, y = 16
Answer: 11 tables of 2, 16 tables of 4

#0950 [System of equations (3 variables)]
Problem: An apple, a banana, and a cherry cup cost $4.05 together. Two apples and a banana cost $3.40. A banana and two cherry cups cost $4.70. Find each price.
Let: a, b, c = prices
Equation: a + b + c = 4.05;  2a + b = 3.4;  b + 2c = 4.7
Solve: Subtract eq 1 from eq 2: a − c = -0.65; substitute into eq 3 → a = 1.1, b = 1.2, c = 1.75
Answer: apple $1.10, banana $1.20, cherry cup $1.75
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0951 [System of equations (3 variables)]
Problem: Three numbers add up to 21. The first plus the second is 10, and the second plus the third is 12. Find all three.
Let: x, y, z = the numbers
Equation: x + y + z = 21;  x + y = 10;  y + z = 12
Solve: z = 21 − 10 = 11; x = 21 − 12 = 9; y = 10 − 9 = 1 → x = 9, y = 1, z = 11
Answer: 9, 1, 11
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0952 [System of equations (3 variables)]
Problem: An apple, a banana, and a cherry cup cost $3.85 together. Two apples and a banana cost $3.05. A banana and two cherry cups cost $4.65. Find each price.

Equation: a + b + c = 3.85;  2a + b = 3.05;  b + 2c = 4.65
Solve: Subtract eq 1 from eq 2: a − c = -0.8; substitute into eq 3 → a = 0.6, b = 1.85, c = 1.4
Answer: apple $0.60, banana $1.85, cherry cup $1.40
----------------------------------------------------------------------

#0959 [Tickets]
Problem: Adult tickets to a movie screening cost $12 and child tickets cost $6. 167 tickets were sold for a total of $1404. How many of each were sold?
Let: x = adult tickets; 167 − x = child tickets
Equation: 12x + 6(167 − x) = 1404
Solve: 6x = 402 → x = 67
Answer: 67 adult and 100 child tickets

#0965 [Work rate]
Problem: Rosa can assemble the shelves in 15 hours. Fatima can do it in 3 hours. How long will it take them working together?
Let: t = hours together
Equation: t/15 + t/3 = 1
Solve: t(18)/45 = 1 → t = 45/18
Answer: 2.5 hours (2.50 h)
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0966 [Work rate]
Problem: Pipe A fills a pool in 4 hours, pipe B in 5 hours, and a drain empties it in 20 hours. With all three open, how long does it take to fill the pool?
Let: t = hours
Equation: t/4 + t/5 − t/20 = 1
Solve: Combine rates → t = 2.5
Answer: 2.5 hours
----------------------------------------------------------------------
#0967 [Work rate]
Problem: A new machine works 2 times as fast as an old one. Together they finish an order in 3 hours. How long would the old machine take alone?
Let: x = old machine's time
Equation: 3/x + 3/(x/2) = 1
Solve: Multiply through by x → x = 9
Answer: 9 hours (new machine: 4.5 hours)
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Answer: 20/9 hours
